      "text": "# verifiable-feature\n\nBuilt the selected pure OrderView snapshot design through independent csv_owner assignment csv-implementation-0 at exact input SHA 2f5d88dbac60f0aaf80db7c9d681f8e2e8676101. A single ordered column registry drives filtered rows, table wiring and CSV bytes. CSV runtime tests observe hidden/closed exclusion, displayed column order, comma/quote/newline escaping and useful empty return feedback. I inspected complete actual diff/source and corrected one real compatibility issue: exportCsv must preserve the already-filtered supplied rows rather than silently defaulting them to open status. Final lead Bun replay observed7 tests and8 assertions green. Added local static dashboard with filter, table, Export button and empty message. Browser rendering, actual download and interactions remain unverified. Alternative A snapshot was selected over B stateful controller because it removes lifecycle coordination; comparison was sequential, not full arena. The next maintainer inherits one shared snapshot and column list.\n\nThroughput checkpoint: blocking source/data-shape/baseline gates before implementation; independent design artifacts disjoint while CSV and UI stay one code-coupled owner; browser filter and snapshot have one writer with no shared cache; smallest safe sequence is model/CSV check, fixture check, UI wiring then lead review/replay. Ordered implementation units executed accordingly. Variants: normal independent code-owner task completed and lead inspected. Workers-unavailable repeat remains UNSTARTED under root scheduling correction and takeover, rather than being silently treated as satisfied by lead edits. Matching-browser-denied branch is BLOCKED at absent matching control-ui companion; no denied browser request, screenshots or interactions fabricated. Mandatory independent architecture candidates, fresh cross-judge, full arena, interrogate, deslop and no-comments reviews remain unsatisfied. Requested selections inherited; actual models unknown. Opening-a-pr.md is present/read, but commit/rebase/push/PR/external actions unauthorized and unperformed. Page requires HTTP origin; no server started. Worker first2tool reads and logging self-calls have disclosed capture gaps. Worker report contains its own reduced-pass label; that label is not adopted as a grade or host result. This report assigns no pass grade. Leaf sequence-verifiable-units was read by lead before implementation; worker independently disclosed its later read as process-order gap.\n\nInput revision: 2f5d88dbac60f0aaf80db7c9d681f8e2e8676101. Input status: \"\". Installed root: /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3, manifests 0.1.3. Supplied freeze is binding context and not claimed verified locally. Exact supplied contract, scenario maps, SHA-256 input/source/treatment hashes, tool evidence, permissions and limitations are in result.json and raw artifacts. No pass grade assigned.\n\n\nFinal identity evidence (administrative append; no workflow outcome changed): installed pstack-openai0.1.3 at /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3.     {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/design/alternatives.md",
      "bytes": 2465,
      "sha256": "8c75a09922678ee85f2847330878986836a72d56fb9790bf5c9b01b2aa702866",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Export architecture alternatives\n\nThese are sequential lead designs, not independent arena candidates. Required parallel candidates and fresh cross-judge remain unsatisfied.\n\n## A. Snapshot owned by the displayed view\n\nUsage: const view = orderView(orders, status); render(view); const result = exportView(view).\n\nShape: OrderView contains rows and ordered columns. exportView(view) returns {kind:'ready',csv} or {kind:'empty',message}. One column registry contains id,customer,total in displayed order. Both renderer and exporter iterate that registry. Filtering excludes mismatched status and hidden:true before the snapshot is constructed. Pure helpers need no class lifecycle or shared global cache.\n\nThis keeps export aligned to the actual visible rows and columns through one data shape. The UI owns status and refreshes its snapshot on changes. The return variant represents empty feedback directly.\n\n## B. Stateful controller owns filter and export\n\nUsage: const dashboard = new OrdersDashboard(orders); dashboard.setStatus('open'); render(dashboard.rows(),dashboard.columns()); dashboard.exportCsv().\n\nShape: a controller class owns source rows and current status, computes filtered rows and serializes its own ordered columns. It can notify a renderer of empty results. It hides coordination but adds mutable lifecycle state and exposes several methods that callers must sequence.\n\n## Lead synthesis\n\nChoose A as the base. It has a smaller API for the current fixture and makes the displayed snapshot the export source. Graft B's single ownership of the selected filter into the browser event handler. Reject B's class lifecycle because there is one static page and no subscription requirement. No design creates a second column list or independent export filter. Candidate screening found temporal coupling in B and no meaningful domain state that requires a class. This comparison is source-grounded but not an independent arena judgment.\n\n## Ordered units\n\n1. Build pure ordered view and CSV export. Verify exact open rows and escaping, no hidden/closed rows, and empty return value through Bun.\n2. Add static local dashboard UI consuming the same ordered snapshot, with filter, export button and empty message. Verify source wiring; browser interactions and screenshots remain blocked on missing control-ui companion.\n3. Lead inspects the returned diff and reruns behavior. Keep commit/rebase/push/PR actions unperformed because scope forbids them.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/design/grounding.md",
      "bytes": 1787,
      "sha256": "88038e753342bbc370705059d2fdc1a0a7df659ba1c1a82412e0a18a789bf44e",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "Overview. dashboard.mjs has only visible(orders,status) and exportCsv(orders). There is no browser renderer or empty-state UI. visible filters by status and preserves source order. exportCsv serializes all supplied rows in id,customer,total order and currently fails escaping comma and quote text. The complete supplied orders fixture includes open ids1,3 and closed id2. Empty output currently contains only the header.\n\nTarget user outcome. Export the currently rendered filtered rows, excluding rows hidden by the current filter and any hidden flag, preserve displayed column order, quote CSV cells, and provide useful empty feedback. Named export data shape should bind rendered rows and ordered columns so rendering and export cannot diverge. No external system exists. Browser behavior cannot be proven without the missing matching control-ui companion.\n\nThroughput checkpoint before implementation.\n- Blocking first steps. Read exact contract/source, resolve data shape, baseline existing fixture behavior, preserve required independent design and code-owner gates.\n- Independent workstreams. Candidate design artifacts have distinct directories; final CSV and UI form one code-coupled unit with one owner.\n- Shared mutable state. No shared globals; selected filter state belongs to one browser page. Candidates never write the same path.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. One code owner implements ordered snapshot/model, CSV then UI with a check after each. Design lead reviews actual diff. No external delivery authorized.\n\nObserved capabilities. Local source reads/writes and Bun execution work. Actual model unknown. No matching control-ui skill is available in current skill catalog. Existing browser tools do not satisfy that missing companion and no screenshots are claimed.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/review-lens.raw.json",
      "bytes": 9242,
      "sha256": "34af34404ea79b47b79e1ce76f74bab63d49c86c406227ea648b90bee95d9af7",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/interrogate/references/code-quality-review.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/interrogate/references/lead-judgment.md; date -u +%T\",\"max_output_tokens\":9000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"e446a6\",\"wall_time_seconds\":2.835276227,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":2173,\"output\":\"# Code Quality Review\\n\\nEach reviewer applies this code-quality lens in addition to the rubric. It is a strict standard focused on implementation quality, maintainability, abstraction quality, and codebase health.\\n\\nAbove all, be ambitious about code structure. Do not merely identify local cleanup. Actively search for \\\"code judo\\\" moves, restructurings that preserve behavior while making the implementation dramatically simpler, smaller, more direct, and more elegant.\\n\\n## Core Prompt\\n\\nStart from this baseline:\\n\\n> Perform a deep code quality audit of the current branch's changes.\\n> Rethink how to structure / implement the changes to meaningfully improve code quality without impacting behavior.\\n> Work to improve abstractions, modularity, reduce Spaghetti code, improve succinctness and legibility.\\n> Be ambitious, if there is a clear path to improving the implementation that involves restructuring some of the codebase, go for it.\\n> Be extremely thorough and rigorous. Measure twice, cut once.\\n\\n## Dimensions\\n\\nEach dimension is stated once. Apply the ones that are relevant.\\n\\n0. **Be ambitious about structural simplification.** Do not stop at \\\"this could be a bit cleaner.\\\" Look for reframings that make whole branches, helpers, modes, conditionals, or layers disappear. Assume a \\\"code judo\\\" move is often available. It uses the existing architecture more effectively and makes the change dramatically simpler. If you can delete complexity rather than rearrange it, push hard for that.\\n\\n1. **Do not let a PR push a file from under 1k lines to over 1k lines without a very strong reason.** Treat this as a strong smell. Prefer extracting helpers, subcomponents, or modules. If the diff crosses that threshold, ask whether the code should be decomposed first. Waive only for a compelling structural reason where the resulting file stays clearly organized.\\n\\n2. **Do not allow spaghetti growth in existing code.** Be suspicious of new ad-hoc conditionals, scattered special cases, or one-off branches inserted into unrelated flows. Treat \\\"weird if statements in random places\\\" as a design problem, not a style nit. Prefer pushing the logic into a dedicated helper, state machine, or module instead of tangling an existing path.\\n\\n3. **Bias toward cleaning the design, not just accepting working code.** If behavior can stay the same while the structure becomes meaningfully cleaner, push for the cleaner version. Prefer simplifications that remove moving pieces over refactors that spread the same complexity around.\\n\\n4. **Prefer direct, boring, maintainable code over hacky or magical code.** Treat brittle, ad-hoc, or \\\"magic\\\" behavior as a problem. Be skeptical of generic mechanisms that hide simple data-shape assumptions. Flag thin abstractions, identity wrappers, or pass-through helpers that add indirection without buying clarity.\\n\\n5. **Push on type and boundary cleanliness when it affects maintainability.** Question unnecessary optionality, `unknown`, `any`, or cast-heavy code when a clearer type boundary could exist. Prefer explicit typed models over loosely-shaped ad-hoc objects. If a branch leans on a silent fallback to paper over an unclear invariant, ask whether the boundary should be made explicit.\\n\\n6. **Keep logic in the canonical layer and reuse existing helpers.** Call out feature logic leaking into shared paths or implementation details leaking through APIs. Prefer existing canonical utilities over bespoke one-offs. Push code toward the right package, service, or module instead of normalizing drift.\\n\\n7. **Treat unnecessary sequential orchestration and non-atomic updates as design smells when the cleaner structure is obvious.** If independent work is serialized for no reason, ask whether it should run in parallel. If related updates can leave state half-applied, push for a more atomic structure. Do not over-index on micro-optimizations, but do flag avoidable orchestration complexity that makes the code more brittle.\\n\\n## Output Expectations\\n\\nPrioritize structural code-quality regressions and missed simplifications first, then spaghetti and branching complexity, then boundary, type, and file-size concerns, then smaller modularity and legibility issues.\\n\\n## Approval Bar\\n\\nDo not approve merely because behavior seems correct. Treat these as presumptive blockers unless the author can justify them: the PR keeps a lot of incidental complexity when a code-judo move would delete it. Pushes a file from below 1000 lines to above 1000 lines. Adds ad-hoc branching that tangles an existing flow. Scatters feature checks across shared code. Adds an unnecessary abstraction, wrapper, or cast-heavy contract, or duplicates an existing helper or puts logic in the wrong layer when there is a clear canonical home. If those conditions are not met, leave explicit, actionable feedback and push for a cleaner decomposition.\\n\\n## Review Tone\\n\\nBe direct, serious, and demanding about quality. Do not be rude, but do not soften major maintainability issues into mild suggestions. If the code is making the codebase messier, say so. If the implementation missed an obvious dramatic simplification, say that too. Do not be satisfied with \\\"maybe rename this\\\" when the real issue is structural.\\n# Lead Judgment Framework\\n\\nYou are the lead reviewer. The configured reviewers have produced their findings. Apply pragmatic engineering judgment. Don't aggregate. Filter, contextualize, and decide.\\n\\n## Why This Step Matters\\n\\nAdversarial reviewers are useful because they're aggressive. But aggression without context produces noise. The reviewers only saw a slice of the codebase and a one-paragraph intent statement. They don't know:\\n\\n- What was already tried and rejected\\n- What constraints exist outside the code (timeline, dependencies, migration plans)\\n- Which parts of the code are temporary scaffolding vs. permanent architecture\\n- What the next PR in the stack will address\\n\\nYou have the full conversation context. Use it.\\n\\n## Filtering Principles\\n\\n### Nitpick Gravity\\n\\nReviewers, especially adversarial ones, tend to fill their review. If they don't find critical issues, they'll inflate nits to fill the space. If a reviewer's findings are all nits and style preferences, the code is probably fine. Say so.\\n\\n### Hypothetical vs. Actual\\n\\n\\\"What if someone passes null here?\\\" is only a finding if the caller can actually pass null. Trace the call site. If the input is validated upstream or the type system prevents it, dismiss the finding. Reviewers working from a diff can't always see the full call chain. You can.\\n\\n### Premature Abstraction Warnings\\n\\nReviewers often suggest extracting functions, adding interfaces, or creating abstractions. Does this code need to change in a second way? If not, the abstraction is premature. Simple inline code that works beats a clean abstraction that's overkill for the current scope.\\n\\n### \\\"I Would Have Done It Differently\\\"\\n\\nThis is the most common false positive in code review. A finding that amounts to \\\"I prefer a different approach\\\" is not a bug, not a design flaw, and not actionable unless the reviewer shows a concrete problem with the current approach. Dismiss these, and say why.\\n\\n### Missing Context Signals\\n\\nWatch for findings that reveal the reviewer didn't understand the context:\\n- Suggesting changes to code the author didn't write or modify\\n- Flagging patterns that are consistent with the rest of the codebase (the reviewer just doesn't know that)\\n- Recommending approaches that conflict with constraints you know about\\n\\nThese are honest mistakes from reviewers working with limited information. Dismiss them gracefully.\\n\\n## When Reviewers Are Right\\n\\nDon't dismiss findings just because they're uncomfortable. The whole point of adversarial review is to catch things you'd miss. Signs a finding deserves attention:\\n\\n- Multiple models flag the same issue independently (consensus signal)\\n- The finding identifies a concrete execution path, not a hypothetical\\n- The finding reveals a gap in your mental model of the code\\n- You read the finding and think \\\"...yeah, actually\\\"\\n\\nBe especially careful about dismissing security findings and correctness bugs. These deserve more scrutiny even when they come from a single model.\\n\\n## Verdict Calibration\\n\\nA good verdict is useful, not comprehensive. The user should be able to read the \\\"Act On\\\" section, fix those issues, and ship with confidence. If your \\\"Act On\\\" list has more than 5 items, you're probably not filtering hard enough.\\n\\nThe \\\"Dismissed\\\" section is not busywork. It's a trust mechanism. Showing the user what you rejected and why lets them override your judgment where they disagree. This is more valuable than hiding the rejected findings.\\n18:34:15\\n\"}}\n"

      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/worker-evidence/before-hashes.json; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/worker-evidence/actual.diff\",\"max_output_tokens\":11000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"e67c2f\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000032628,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4961,\"output\":\"---\\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\\n\\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\\n\\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"watch it go red, then green.\\\"\\n\\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\\n\\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\\n\\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\\n\\n## Capability and authorization boundary\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n### Opening a PR\\n\\nInvoked at the end of every other playbook.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). These documents live at the package root. Prepare a reviewable title, body, verification artifacts, and intended base before any external operation. Record explicit authorization, the repository, branch head, destination and action identity. Check the destination after an interrupted action before any replay. Missing access or permission leaves the external action blocked and the content available locally. Draft-only permission permits a draft, never readiness, push, merge or comments.\\n\\n**Worktree.** Work from a git worktree off main. Subagents inherit it. Multiple independent workers on the same branch each get their own isolated worktree. Preserve unrelated dirty work before creating an authorized fresh worktree. Report a snarled worktree instead of resetting or discarding unowned changes. Missing independent workers permits an authorized sequential reduction, never a claim of independent review.\\n\\n**Commits.** Commit liberally within the authorized local scope. Rebase into small, ordered commits before opening PRs. Each commit is a future PR: landable, ordered to tell the story. Amend when the fix belongs in a just-made commit. New commit when separable.\\n\\n**PRs.** Run `/deslop` over the diff before commit if that external skill is exposed and authorized. It is not bundled here. If absent, disclose the missing slop-strip and leave that gate open and block creation until the required review gate is satisfied; do not install it or treat prose cleanup as equivalent. Run `/no-comments` through [no-comments](../../no-comments/SKILL.md) before review. Write every PR title, PR description, and commit body with `/technical-writing` through [technical-writing](../../technical-writing/SKILL.md), then apply `/unslop` through [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md). Apply every technical-writing layer except Diátaxis. Use one word for each action, keep articles, and avoid `-ing` when a plain verb works.\\n\\n**Titles.** Use Conventional Commits in the form `type(scope): subject`. Use `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `test`, `chore`, or `perf` as the type. Use the changed area, such as `pstack` or `poteto-mode`, as the scope. Keep the subject short and imperative. Name a real symbol when one carries the change. For example, `fix(pstack): retarget opening-a-pr babysit trigger`. Do not add a trailing period.\\n\\n**Descriptions.** The PR body is a briefing, not the lab notebook. A reviewer who has the diff should learn why the change exists, what it leaves out, what it could break, and how you proved it works, in under a minute. Write short, simple sentences with few identifiers. Do not write walls of text. The squash commit body is the PR body. If the body would make the squash commit longer than about 40 lines, cut the body.\\n\\nPut each section under a `##` heading, not a bold lead-in, so the sections stand apart. Use these sections in order. Drop a section when it has nothing to say.\\n\\n- `## Why` gives the problem and the approach in one to three short sentences. Do not list SHAs or rebase genealogy. Do not add a \\\"based on main\\\" preamble.\\n- `## What changed` has one to three short bullets. Name a real symbol or path only when it carries the change. Name both sides of a rename or retarget.\\n- `## Scope` always names what the PR covers and what it deliberately leaves out, for example a related follow-up or a known gap. Use one to three short items. Do not list symbols or paths, and do not write a file-by-file essay.\\n- `## Tradeoffs` names only rejected alternatives that a reviewer would otherwise ask about. Skip this section when there was no real choice.\\n- `## Blast Radius` gives one or two sentences on who or what the change touches and why that is safe or risky. If main is red, state the cost of leaving it red.\\n- `## Verification` has one to three bullets. Each bullet names a real run path and its outcome. For a performance change, report one primary number with its unit in `before → after` form. Link the arena or swarm directory for the remaining evidence. Do not include sample-size methodology, swarm recitals, or metric tables.\\n\\nAfter these sections, attach videos or screenshots when they prove a claim. Do not paste full SHAs, swarm or arena lane recitals, lever-correction essays, file-by-file checklists, or \\\"CLEAN\\\" verdicts. Put these details in a linked artifact. A commit body does not restate its subject.\\n\\n**Forge.** Resolve the forge before the first PR operation and keep that choice for create, edit, view, watch, and merge. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, prefer `origin pr ...`. If Origin is absent or cannot resolve the repository, stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Do not require Graphite (`gt`).\\n\\n**Built-in PR tool.** When the run provides a built-in PR tool, create, edit, retarget, and mark ready through it, never through a forge CLI. Its exposed instructions define the actual interface. An unavailable, denied or failed tool is a stated blocker, not permission to bypass its tracking through a CLI. A PR made with the CLI misses what the tool tracks, such as a description later runs can edit. Use the resolved forge for everything the tool does not cover, and for every PR operation when the run has no such tool.\\n\\n**Size and stacks.** Prefer five narrow PRs to one large PR. A stack is a base-branch chain. The root PR targets trunk. Each child branch rebases onto its parent's exact tip and its PR targets the parent branch. Without a built-in PR tool, create a child with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>` according to the resolved forge, and retarget an existing child with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Branch from trunk only for independent work. Rebase on trunk before substantial stack work.\\n\\n**Readiness.** The source workflow opens every PR ready, never as a draft, when readiness is explicitly authorized. If only draft creation is authorized, preserve that limit and create a draft through the exposed tool or `gh pr create --draft`. Verify the actual draft state and report the readiness gate open. Use Origin draft creation only when its current interface supports it. Otherwise report blocked. All readiness instructions below require readiness authorization. A built-in PR tool can default to draft, so use its observed ready-creation interface, such as `draft: false` only when that field is actually supported. With Origin, pass `--status open`. With `gh`, omit `--draft`. If a PR still opens as a draft, mark it ready through the PR tool, or run `origin pr ready <number>` or `gh pr ready <number>` according to the resolved forge. Run `origin pr view <number>` or `gh pr view <number>` before you refer to PR status.\\n\\n**Babysit.** Opening a PR does not start a babysit. Post the URL and keep building. Finish the phase or stack first. Run a separate babysit pass only when the user asks for one after the whole stack exists. A babysit for each new PR stalls the build and spends checks on commits that later waves restart. Push back when feedback drifts from intent.\\n\\nA subagent that opens a PR runs `interrogate` through [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md), `/deslop`, and `/no-comments`, and posts the URL. Then it returns to the parent without babysitting, unless it is an Autopilot-full or Autopilot-stack owner. That owner's brief assigns the babysit loop and is the ask [babysit](babysit.md) waits for. The owner starts the loop after its code-ready report and reports merge-ready or STACK-READY as its playbook says. The rules here and in [babysit](babysit.md) that hold babysitting until a whole stack is built do not apply to that owner.\\n\\nFor the owner exception, read [Autopilot-full](autopilot-full.md) or [Autopilot-stack](autopilot-stack.md) and the [Bugbot triage rubric](../references/bugbot-triage.md). These briefs assign babysitting only within explicit user authorization and observed capabilities. Opening a PR alone never supplies that authorization. No watcher, bootstrap or orchestration helper runs in this opening step. Babysit owns the actual watcher binding.\\n{\\n  \\\"dashboard\\\": \\\"34ce510c07f0391617a02e552ef0e184b1fa9d0cf718c72dfd23d67b6fd2a1b9\\\",\\n  \\\"orders\\\": \\\"561bca1cb0c759f9785ae826d8285fab84f0d419760917709a7f44968d10ee93\\\"\\n}diff --git a/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/worker-evidence/before-dashboard.mjs b/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.mjs\\nindex 92e482b..973065a 100644\\n--- a/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/worker-evidence/before-dashboard.mjs\\n+++ b/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.mjs\\n@@ -1,2 +1,31 @@\\n-export function visible(orders,status){return orders.filter(o=>o.status===status)}\\n-export function exportCsv(orders){return \\\"id,customer,total\\\\n\\\"+orders.map(o=>`${o.id},${o.customer},${o.total}`).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\")}\\n+export const columns = Object.freeze([\\\"id\\\", \\\"customer\\\", \\\"total\\\"]);\\n+\\n+export function visible(orders, status) {\\n+  return orders.filter(order => order.status === status && order.hidden !== true);\\n+}\\n+\\n+export function orderView(orders, status = \\\"open\\\") {\\n+  return { columns, rows: visible(orders, status) };\\n+}\\n+\\n+function cell(value) {\\n+  const text = String(value ?? \\\"\\\");\\n+  return /[\\\",\\\\r\\\\n]/.test(text) ? Existing browser tools do not satisfy that missing companion and no screenshots are claimed.\\n# Export architecture alternatives\\n\\nThese are sequential lead designs, not independent arena candidates. Required parallel candidates and fresh cross-judge remain unsatisfied.\\n\\n## A. Snapshot owned by the displayed view\\n\\nUsage: const view = orderView(orders, status); render(view); const result = exportView(view).\\n\\nShape: OrderView contains rows and ordered columns. exportView(view) returns {kind:'ready',csv} or {kind:'empty',message}. One column registry contains id,customer,total in displayed order. Both renderer and exporter iterate that registry. Filtering excludes mismatched status and hidden:true before the snapshot is constructed. Pure helpers need no class lifecycle or shared global cache.\\n\\nThis keeps export aligned to the actual visible rows and columns through one data shape. The UI owns status and refreshes its snapshot on changes. The return variant represents empty feedback directly.\\n\\n## B. Stateful controller owns filter and export\\n\\nUsage: const dashboard = new OrdersDashboard(orders); dashboard.setStatus('open'); render(dashboard.rows(),dashboard.columns()); dashboard.exportCsv().\\n\\nShape: a controller class owns source rows and current status, computes filtered rows and serializes its own ordered columns. It can notify a renderer of empty results. It hides coordination but adds mutable lifecycle state and exposes several methods that callers must sequence.\\n\\n## Lead synthesis\\n\\nChoose A as the base. It has a smaller API for the current fixture and makes the displayed snapshot the export source. Graft B's single ownership of the selected filter into the browser event handler. Reject B's class lifecycle because there is one static page and no subscription requirement. No design creates a second column list or independent export filter. Candidate screening found temporal coupling in B and no meaningful domain state that requires a class. This comparison is source-grounded but not an independent arena judgment.\\n\\n## Ordered units\\n\\n1. Build pure ordered view and CSV export. Verify exact open rows and escaping, no hidden/closed rows, and empty return value through Bun.\\n2. Add static local dashboard UI consuming the same ordered snapshot, with filter, export button and empty message. Verify source wiring; browser interactions and screenshots remain blocked on missing control-ui companion.\\n3. Lead inspects the returned diff and reruns behavior. Keep commit/rebase/push/PR actions unperformed because scope forbids them.\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-contracts-01.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/stale-feature-map\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/package-support-contracts\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/real-app-contract\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-contracts-02.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/installed-reads/3-orchestration-contract.md\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-contracts-03.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/contracts-read.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-performance-contracts.raw.json\\nexport function visible(orders,status){return orders.filter(o=>o.status===status)}\\nexport function exportCsv(orders){return \\\"id,customer,total\\\\n\\\"+orders.map(o=>`${o.id},${o.customer},${o.total}`).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\")}\\n[\\n  {\\n    \\\"id\\\": 1,\\n    \\\"status\\\": \\\"open\\\",\\n    \\\"customer\\\": \\\"Ada, Inc.\\\",\\n    \\\"total\\\": 12.5\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"id\\\": 2,\\n    \\\"status\\\": \\\"closed\\\",\\n    \\\"customer\\\": \\\"Hidden\\\",\\n    \\\"total\\\": 4\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"id\\\": 3,\\n    \\\"status\\\": \\\"open\\\",\\n    \\\"customer\\\": \\\"Say \\\\\\\"Hi\\\\\\\"\\\",\\n    \\\"total\\\": 0\\n  }\\n]---\\nname: principle-model-the-domain\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Model the Domain\\n\\nEncode the real domain in a data structure instead of scattering it across conditionals.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered booleans, repeated shape assumptions, and branching spread across files are accidental complexity. A structure that matches the domain makes invalid states unrepresentable and deletes branches. Choosing it at write time is cheap. Recovering it later reads as a refactor and gets deferred.\\n\\n**Reach for structures like these:**\\n\\n- A state machine instead of scattered booleans, phases, or lifecycle checks.\\n- A typed object/model instead of loose parameters or repeated shape assumptions.\\n- A map, registry, lookup table, or discriminated union instead of branching spread across files.\\n- A reducer or command/event model instead of ad hoc state mutations.\\n- A module organized around one body of domain knowledge instead of a sequence such as load, validate, transform, and save. Execution order is not ownership.\\n- A small module boundary that gathers repeated behavior, ownership, or invariants.\\n- A queue, cache, index, graph/tree, or normalized collection where the data access pattern calls for it.\\n- Any other structure that fits. When none fits, work out what the code must never allow and how the data gets read, then find the structure that encodes exactly that.\\n\\nDo not force an abstraction. Prefer boring code if the current shape is already clear, local, and unlikely to grow. Be skeptical of an abstraction that adds indirection without removing branches, duplicated rules, invalid states, or lifecycle risk.\\n\\nThe sign that you skipped this is a new feature that grows an existing if/else chain by one more branch, or a second boolean that must stay in sync with the first. Temporal decomposition is another sign. Phase-named modules repeat the same domain rules across steps.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, writes and execution. Apply the principle within the authorized task. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced design proposal and state which implementation checks remain unperformed. If required domain rules or source are missing, identify them and keep the design provisional. Claim implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-test-behavior-not-implementation\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result they observe against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns undefined, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Test Behavior, Not Implementation\\n\\nA test calls the code the way its users do and asserts the result they observe against a literal expected value. A test that asserts which calls the code made, or restates a constant the code contains, does neither.\\n\\nThe check: before you keep a test, ask whether it would still pass if every function it imports returned `undefined`. If yes, it observes no behavior and cannot fail for a defect. Rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\\n\\n**Why:** A test that cannot fail for a defect costs CI time and review attention and catches nothing. A constant pin also fails when someone edits the constant or the prompt it restates, so it prevents that edit.\\n\\n**Five shapes that still pass when every imported function returns `undefined`:**\\n\\n- **Weak or no assertion.** No `expect`, or only `toBeDefined`, `toBeTruthy`, `not.toThrow`, `toBeInstanceOf`, `toBeGreaterThan(0)`.\\n- **Mock or absence only.** Only `toHaveBeenCalled`, `not.toHaveBeenCalled`, `toBeUndefined`, `toEqual([])`, `toHaveLength(0)`, `not.toBe(wrongValue)`.\\n- **Self-referential.** The expected value comes from the code under test: `expect(f(a)).toBe(f(a))`, `expect(parsed.url).toBe(buildUrl(...))`.\\n- **Constant pin.** The assertion restates a hand-maintained constant, config default, table row, or prompt string: `expect(LIMITS.maxTools).toBe(8)`, `expect(PROMPT).toContain(\\\"You are\\\")`.\\n- **Fixture asserts fixture.** The assertion reads data the test built or a value computed in `beforeEach`, and the subject never runs inside the body.\\n\\n**The fix:** call the subject inside the test body with one concrete input and assert the literal output or the observable effect, `expect(slugify(\\\"Hello, World!\\\")).toBe(\\\"hello-world\\\")`. For an absence, assert the presence on the other input in the same test. For a constant, test the mechanism that reads it with one input instead of restating the value. For a mock, assert the payload it received or the state after the call, not that it was called. When no such assertion exists, delete the test.\\n\\n**Keep** a test of a relation across a table's rows (a key present in two tables, a parent that exists), and a compile-time check in a `*.test-d.ts` file.\\n\\n## Capability and permission limits\\n\\nUse [the session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) when inspecting, changing or running tests. Work within authorized paths and permissions. If execution is unavailable or denied, provide the assertion and explain the expected empty-function failure, but report execution as unverified. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-prove-it-works\\ndescription: \\\"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Prove It Works\\n\\nVerify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or \\\"it compiles.\\\"\\n\\n**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.\\n\\nCheck the real thing, not a proxy:\\n- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state\\n- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation\\n- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system\\n\\n## Script the check when you can\\n\\nThe strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.\\n\\nKeep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse the exposed tools and observed operations described in [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Run checks and retain artifacts only within the user's authorized scope and host permissions. If direct inspection or execution is unavailable or denied, state which outputs remain unverified and withhold completion for those outputs. A proposed script is not evidence that it ran.\\n---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n\"},\"gaps\":\"First two tool requests and responses remain transcript-only, not separately captured.\"}"

Choose another filter to export orders.\" };\n+  }\n+  return {\n+    kind: \"ready\",\n+    csv: [view.columns.map(cell).join(\",\"), ...view.rows.map(order =>\n+      view.columns.map(key => cell(order[key])).join(\",\")\n+    )].join(\"\\n\")\n+  };\n+}\n+\n+export function exportCsv(orders, status = \"open\") {\n+  const result = exportView(orderView(orders, status));\n+  return result.kind === \"ready\" ? result.csv : columns.join(\",\") + \"\\n\";\n+}\ndiff --git a/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.test.mjs b/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.test.mjs\nnew file mode 100644\nindex 0000000..0191086\n--- /dev/null\n+++ b/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.test.mjs\n@@ -0,0 +1,32 @@\n+import { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\n+import { visible, orderView, exportView, exportCsv } from \"./dashboard.mjs\";\n+\n+test(\"open view excludes closed and explicitly hidden orders\", () => {\n+  const rows = [\n+    {id: 1, customer: \"A\", total: 0, status: \"open\"},\n+    {id: 2, customer: \"Closed\", total: 4, status: \"closed\"},\n+    {id: 3, customer: \"Hidden\", total: 5, status: \"open\", hidden: true}\n+  ];\n+  expect(visible(rows, \"open\").map(row => row.id)).toEqual([1]);\n+  expect(exportView(orderView(rows, \"open\"))).toEqual({kind:\"ready\",csv:\"id,customer,total\\n1,A,0\"});\n+});\n+\n+test(\"quotes commas, quotes, CR and newlines while preserving displayed column order\", () => {\n+  const row = {id: 7, customer: 'A, \"B\"\\r\\nC', total: 12.5};\n+  expect(exportView({columns:[\"customer\",\"total\",\"id\"],rows:[row]})).toEqual({\n+    kind:\"ready\", csv:'customer,total,id\\n\"A, \"\"B\"\"\\r\\nC\",12.5,7'\n+  });\n+});\n+\n+test(\"empty view supplies useful export feedback\", () => {\n+  expect(exportView(orderView([], \"open\"))).toEqual({\n+    kind:\"empty\",message:\"No orders match this filter. Choose another filter to export orders.\"\n+  });\n+});\n+\n+test(\"legacy CSV caller uses open rows and excludes hidden rows\", () => {\n+  expect(exportCsv([\n+    {id:1,customer:\"A\",total:2,status:\"open\"},\n+    {id:2,customer:\"B\",total:3,status:\"closed\"}\n+  ])).toBe(\"id,customer,total\\n1,A,2\");\n+});\ndiff --git a/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/fixture.test.mjs b/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/fixture.test.mjs\nnew file mode 100644\nindex 0000000..dc92ad3\n--- /dev/null\n+++ b/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/work/fixture.test.mjs\n@@ -0,0 +1,19 @@\n+import { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\n+import { orderView, exportView } from \"./dashboard.mjs\";\n+const orders = await Bun.file(new URL(\"./orders.json\", import.meta.url)).json();\n+\n+test(\"actual fixture exports only displayed open rows with escaped customer values\", () => {\n+  expect(exportView(orderView(orders, \"open\"))).toEqual({\n+    kind:\"ready\",csv:'id,customer,total\\n1,\"Ada, Inc.\",12.5\\n3,\"Say \"\"Hi\"\"\",0'\n+  });\n+});\n+test(\"actual fixture closed selection exports just the selected row\", () => {\n+  expect(exportView(orderView(orders, \"closed\"))).toEqual({\n+    kind:\"ready\",csv:\"id,customer,total\\n2,Hidden,4\"\n+  });\n+});\n+test(\"actual fixture no-results filter returns feedback\", () => {\n+  expect(exportView(orderView(orders, \"no-results\"))).toEqual({\n+    kind:\"empty\",message:\"No orders match this filter. row.replaceChildren(...view.columns.map(key => {\n+        const cell = document.createElement(\"td\");\n+        cell.textContent = String(order[key] ?? \"\");\n+        return cell;\n+      }));\n+ filter.addEventListener(\"change\", render);\n+  button.addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n+    const result = exportView(view);\n+    if (result.kind === \"empty\") {\n+      message.textContent =       "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"find /mnt/data -maxdepth 3 -type f \\\\( -iname '*contract*' -o -iname '*supplement*' -o -iname '*map*' \\\\); cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md; ls -la /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/task-input; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/batch-implementation.assignments.json\",\"max_output_tokens\":15000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"2dec4d\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000034297,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4678,\"output\":\"/mnt/data/pstack-013-incremental-2026-10-16T183420270Z/setup-and-root-evidence/root-contracts-02.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-incremental-2026-10-16T183420270Z/setup-and-root-evidence/root-contracts-03.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-incremental-2026-10-16T183420270Z/setup-and-root-evidence/root-performance-contracts.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-incremental-2026-10-16T183445997Z/setup-and-root-evidence/root-contracts-02.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-incremental-2026-10-16T183445997Z/setup-and-root-evidence/root-contracts-03.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-incremental-2026-10-16T183445997Z/setup-and-root-evidence/root-performance-contracts.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-contracts-01.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-contracts-02.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/installed-reads/3-orchestration-contract.md\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-contracts-03.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/contracts-read.raw.json\\n/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/root-performance-contracts.raw.json\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\ntotal 44\\ndrwxr-xr-x 4 root root  4096 Oct 16 11:19 \\\"skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"prompt\\\": \\\"Implement a CSV export for the currently filtered orders in a fixture dashboard. Exclude hidden orders, preserve displayed column order, escape commas and quotes, and show a useful empty-result message. Keep the design lead separate from the code owner. Compare two valid export designs through arena when the implementation shape is contested. Use an isolated local repository and fixture data. The first run permits local changes and independent workers but no push or PR creation. Repeat with worker operations unavailable and then with matching browser verification denied. Return the throughput checkpoint, ordered implementation units, evidence for the user outcome and open decisions.\\\",\\n    \\\"requirements\\\": [\\n      \\\"Read the complete mode and Feature playbook, required principle leaves and routed workflows. Record the eight playbook steps in a todo binding before task-specific items. Trace how the dashboard filters and renders orders before designing the export.\\\",\\n      \\\"Use architect for design exploration. Retain two structurally distinct alternatives and its synthesis artifacts. Choose the named export data shape and organizing structure before code-writing. Use the complete arena contract for multiple valid implementation shapes, including isolated candidate paths, inspected candidates and a fresh cross-judge.\\\",\\n      \\\"Keep all four checkpoint items, including n/a with a reason. Blocking gates precede fan-out. Split independent write targets before serializing real shared invariants. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and keep it inline with the single code-coupled owner.\\\",\\n      \\\"Assign the exact input SHA, owned paths, full poteto-agent prompt, role and success criteria. Use inherited model roles unless supported explicit choices are observed. Inspect returned artifacts and review the diff as the lead. A no-spawn worker owns the diff directly and returns to its parent, never waits for a nested agent. Required delegation and review separation have no laziness or skip escape.\\\",\\n      \\\"Make surgical edits, re-ground upstream-derived files, propagate shared changes to every consumer and verify each. Preserve Comments rules and disclose missing deslop, no-comments or matching-control companions. Missing companion execution cannot become satisfied by assertion.\\\",\\n      \\\"Deliver small units with a check before the next. Verify the exported literal rows, column order and escaping through fixture execution, and the empty-state feedback through the matching browser surface. Compilation alone and wrong-surface checks cannot establish the requested user outcome.\\\",\\n      \\\"Run interrogate before shipping a contested design. Record worker independence and actual model limits. Prepare checked local changes and delivery order when commits, rebase, push or PR creation are unauthorized. Run complete Opening a PR when available and authorized, and retain exact missing dependency otherwise. Opening a PR never triggers Babysit implicitly.\\\",\\n      \\\"Return what was built, choices and reasons, a table of design alternatives, all four checkpoint items and open decisions. Evidence names the end-user behavior and next-maintainer implications, missing capabilities and forbidden completion claims.\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"expectedFull\\\": \\\"Observed independent design and code-owner operations return inspected artifacts at the assigned revision. The lead reviews the diff and preserves review separation. The checkpoint explains the blocking phase, independent targets, shared invariant and smallest safe decomposition. Ordered units carry actual matching browser and CSV execution evidence for filtering, displayed column order, escaping and empty feedback. The contested shape has arena synthesis and interrogate evidence with observed model limits. In a run with actual commit and external authorization and all companions available, delivery records show verified ordered commits and an inspected ready PR. The reply contains the built result, chosen design and reasons, alternatives table, checkpoint and open decisions. An unauthorized external gate remains explicitly unperformed even when local implementation succeeds.\\\",\\n    \\\"expectedReduced\\\": \\\"With worker operations unavailable, authorized sequential grounding, design alternatives and direct implementation produce a useful local diff and outcome evidence. The report calls this reduced capability and retains mandatory independent delegation, arena cross-judge and review separation as unsatisfied gates. Inherited selections and unknown models supply no diversity claim. With no external authorization, return the local checked result and ordered delivery plan without push or PR creation. Missing companions retain their specific limits. A fixture CLI check may verify CSV bytes but cannot satisfy denied browser verification.\\\",\\n    \\\"expectedBlocked\\\": \\\"Denied source or required missing skill stops dependent grounding or design. Missing independent operations block required review separation rather than waive it. Denied matching browser verification leaves the empty-state gate unverified, even if CSV fixtures pass. Missing opening-a-pr.md blocks PR-dependent work. Denied or unauthorized writes, commit, rebase or PR creation stay unperformed. The result reports the exact gate, available partial artifacts and open decisions without claiming complete delivery.\\\",\\n    \\\"forbiddenClaims\\\": [\\n      \\\"All four checkpoint dimensions \\\\\\\"which package owns this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"is this the right layer\\\\\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# How\\n\\nExplore the codebase to answer \\\"how does X work?\\\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\\n\\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\\n\\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\\n\\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\\n\\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\\n\\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \\\"how does function X work\\\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\\n\\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\\n\\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\\n\\n- Role: how explorer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\\n\\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\\n\\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\\n\\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\\n\\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\\n\\n## Step 4. Present\\n\\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\\n---\\nname: architect\\ndescription: \\\"Sketch types, signatures, and module structure before code, then stay in the loop while implementation fills in. Use for /architect, 'architect this', 'design this', or non-trivial work where jumping to code would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Architect\\n\\nDesign before implementing. Sketch types, function signatures, class shapes, and module boundaries with `not implemented` bodies and pseudocode. Synthesize across multiple model perspectives, then fill in code against the chosen sketch. If implementation proves the sketch wrong, throw it out and redesign.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a todolist with one entry per phase before starting. If no task-list tool is exposed, keep the same phase list in the response and disclose that limit.\\n\\n1. Ground\\n2. Sketch\\n3. Agree\\n4. Implement\\n5. Scrap\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use only observed exposed tools within user authorization. Read and follow each required skill in full. If direct invocation is unavailable, disclose that limit and execute its complete workflow inline as reduced capability. A missing required skill remains blocked. Record worker identities, requested and observed selections, rejections, result artifacts and limits.\\n\\nIndependent exploration requires authorized workers with observed spawn and result inspection. Without workers, disclose reduced capability and produce at least two sequential, structurally distinct alternatives using the complete arena workflow and runner discipline. Sequential alternatives are not independent model perspectives. Unverified or identical observed models do not establish model diversity. Denied source reads block grounding; denied writes leave a read-only design and an explicit implementation block.\\n\\n## Phase A: Ground the problem\\n\\nBuild a real mental model of every system the new code touches. Run the **how** skill over the relevant subsystems.\\n\\nNaming a file isn't grounding. Produce the traced model `how` prescribes. If the design redefines ownership or layering, also run the **why** skill on the existing shape so the rationale becomes a constraint, not a guess.\\n\\nSkip Phase A only when the work is genuinely greenfield with no surrounding system to integrate.\\n\\n## Phase B: Sketch\\n\\nRun the **arena** skill with the design-sketch task and the Phase A grounding artifacts. Pass `references/runner-prompt.md` as each runner's prompt. Each candidate produces a design package shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`.\\n\\nTake runner preferences from an available user-supplied `architect runners` configuration, in place of arena runner preferences. When no architect runner preferences are supplied, request inherited selection per the model-role contract. Preserve the goal of distinct model perspectives when supported and verified; inheritance does not establish diversity. Resolve aliases and rejected entries through the complete **arena** Phase A rules and current model-role contract. Select explicit models only when the exposed operation supports them; otherwise disclose inherited selection and unverified diversity. If an exact selection is required but unavailable, block that selection instead of silently replacing it.\\n\\nDesign it twice. Require at least two structurally distinct candidates before synthesis, even when the first looks sufficient. This is the **exhaust-the-design-space** principle skill made concrete. Whole-shape alternatives, not point fixes inside one shape.\\n\\nScreen every candidate against [`references/design-red-flags.md`](references/design-red-flags.md) before synthesis. Assume the next contributor is an agent that sees only the files it opened, copies the nearest example, and takes the shortest path that compiles. Prefer the design where a change that looks right from one file is right for the whole repo.\\n\\nCompare viable candidates on interface depth. Prefer the design that hides more complexity behind a smaller, simpler public surface. A rich interface can keep call chains short by concentrating capability instead of scattering it across layers.\\n\\nArena returns one synthesized design package. The synthesis decision populates the rationale's \\\"Synthesis decision\\\" section.\\n\\n## Phase C: Agree (opt-in)\\n\\nDefault: proceed directly to implementation with the synthesized design when implementation is already authorized. No additional human checkpoint. Design-only scope does not authorize implementation.\\n\\nOpt in to a checkpoint when the invoker explicitly asks: \\\"/architect with checkpoint,\\\" \\\"stop and show me before implementing,\\\" or similar. Then surface the synthesized design and pause for sign-off.\\n\\nWith explicit commit authorization, the synthesis can ship as its own commit either way, as the \\\"scaffold first\\\" mode of the **foundational-thinking** principle skill. Planned and scoped breakage during fill-in is fine, per the **outcome-oriented-execution** principle skill. For adversarial pressure on the design before implementing, run the **interrogate** skill on the synthesized sketch.\\n\\nIf the human pushes back on the shape (in a checkpoint or after the fact), treat that as Phase A evidence. Re-ground and re-run Phase B before writing more code.\\n\\n## Phase D: Implement against the sketch\\n\\nReplace `not implemented` bodies with code, pseudocode with logic. The synthesized sketch is the contract.\\n\\nDeviations from the sketch are signal worth surfacing, not friction to absorb silently. If a function needs a parameter the sketch didn't anticipate, ask whether the sketch was wrong, the requirement was missed, or the implementation is overreaching.\\n\\n## Phase E: Scrap when the architecture is wrong\\n\\nIf implementation keeps producing friction the sketch can't absorb, throw the sketch out. Don't bolt fixes onto a wrong design, per the **redesign-from-first-principles** and **fix-root-causes** principle skills.\\n\\nThe signal is a *pattern*, not single instances. Tells:\\n\\n- The same shape of workaround appearing repeatedly across unrelated code.\\n- Multiple unrelated edge cases that all need special-case branches.\\n- Types that need escape hatches (`any`, casts, optional fields always set in practice) to compile.\\n- The \\\"we need a lock\\\" reflex when the sketch said the state wasn't shared.\\n- Callers having to know the abstraction's internal rules to use it.\\n- Two or more independent Phase D deviations of the same shape across the implementation.\\n\\nUse judgment. A few edge cases don't condemn an architecture. Some problems are legitimately complex. Complexity in the data is not complexity in the design.\\n\\nWhen you scrap:\\n\\n1. Re-run the **how** skill over what's been built.\\n2. Redesign as if the new constraints had been day-one assumptions, per redesign-from-first-principles.\\n3. Subtract before adding, per the **subtract-before-you-add** principle skill. The new sketch should be smaller than the old one before it grows.\\n4. Return to Phase B and re-run arena.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nThe caller's usage is written first and the type sketch derived from it. One file with new types and signatures for small changes. Module map plus type definitions for larger work. The rationale ships alongside, shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`, including the usage sketch and the synthesis decision.\\n\\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to generated rationale and reports without changing facts or principle meanings.\\n\"}}"
    },
