      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/arena/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/interrogate/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/interrogate/references/reviewer-prompt.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/interrogate/references/rubric.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/task-input/inputs/dashboard.mjs /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/task-input/inputs/orders.json\",\"max_output_tokens\":17000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"bd1d54\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000043429,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":5912,\"output\":\"---\\nname: arena\\ndescription: \\\"Spawn N parallel candidates at the same task, pick a base, graft the strongest parts of the losers into it. Use for /arena, 'arena this', 'throw it in the arena', or when one attempt at a non-trivial artifact would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Arena\\n\\nRead [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Use only exposed, authorized operations. Read each applied principle leaf in full before dependent work. Missing required reads block that work. Invocation aliases are guidance, not proof of registered host commands.\\n\\nIndependent candidates and a fresh independent judge require observed worker operations and inspected artifacts. If unavailable, produce authorized sequential alternatives and parent comparison only as reduced capability. Do not claim independence, background execution, model diversity or a full arena pass. If independence is required, leave that gate blocked.\\n\\nFan out N parallel attempts at the same task. Read every candidate end to end. Pick the strongest as the base. Graft the best ideas from the others into it. Verify the synthesized result.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen an exposed todo list with one entry per phase before launching anything. Without one, use an authorized local Markdown checklist and disclose the reduced binding.\\n\\n1. Frame\\n2. Fan out\\n3. Cross-judge\\n4. Pick\\n5. Graft\\n6. Verify\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\nThe N candidates will receive the same prompt, so the prompt is the contract.\\n\\n1. State the artifact each candidate is producing.\\n2. Derive the rubric. State what success looks like for *this* task, then turn it into 3-6 concrete gradeable criteria. The rubric is the picker's tool in Phase D. Candidates only see the task.\\n3. Pick the runners. Use an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena runners` preference if available. Otherwise request two inherited workers, one per seat. The source defaults used two specific model families. Their source-host slugs are not portable availability evidence. Select an explicit model or effort only from current exposed documentation or observed enumeration under the model-role contract. An `auto` or `inherit-parent` entry means omit explicit model selection. On rejection, record it and disclose a supported same-family alternative when available, otherwise an authorized inherited alternative. If the exact model is required, block instead. Record requested and observed model and effort separately, using unknown when unobserved. More seats can cover multiple design directions. Same model N times is appropriate when work is generation-bound rather than judgment-sensitive. Unknown models leave diversity unverified.\\n4. Assign output paths. Each candidate writes to its own authorized location, a git worktree where supported, otherwise a distinct local candidate directory. Follow [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Confirm isolation and write permissions before dispatch. Record immutable assignment identity, input SHA, owner, role and evidence links under the assignment contract. Workers never integrate shared files. Worktree creation and persistence require observed authorized capabilities.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nDispatch all N independent candidates together through the exposed worker operation, using observed background execution when available. Give each the same task, shared grounding path, its isolated output path, and instructions to produce both the artifact and a short rationale. Withhold the rubric from candidates. Inspect terminal results and artifact paths before accepting completion; a wait notification alone is insufficient.\\n\\nEach rationale names the alternatives the candidate considered and what it rejected.\\n\\nIf a candidate fails to produce output, proceed with N-1 and note the dropout in the synthesis record. One survivor means incomplete competition, even if its artifact is useful. Zero survivors blocks synthesis. Never integrate partial or cancelled artifacts as completed candidates.\\n\\n## Phase C: Cross-judge\\n\\nAfter all Phase B candidates reach terminal state and their accepted artifacts are inspected, choose one model from an explicitly supplied, authorized `arena cross-judge pool` preference. Without that preference, use an inherited worker or a supported explicit selection under the model-role contract. The source pool named the same two families as the runners; it does not establish portable model availability. Prefer a different observed model family from the parent's when available. Disclose rejected choices and supported fallbacks, or block if the exact choice is required.\\n\\nSpawn one fresh readonly judge worker. It sees the rubric and completed candidates by path label, scores each criterion, and recommends a base with rationale. It runs in parallel with the parent's reading in Phase D when observed background execution permits it, never with candidates still writing. The judge has no candidate write ownership. Unknown actual models leave diversity unverified. Unavailable fresh judging remains a reduced or blocked gate, never an invented independent verdict.\\n\\n## Phase D: Pick a base\\n\\nRead every candidate end to end before picking.\\n\\nScore each candidate against the rubric criterion by criterion, not on holistic feel. Compare against the cross-judge. Agreement on the base confirms the pick. Disagreement means one of you is biased or the rubric was ambiguous. Read both rationales before deciding.\\n\\nPick the base on which candidate a future maintainer can extend most easily without breaking invariants. Prefer the cleaner boundary or smaller API when two feel tied, per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md).\\n\\nRecord the pick and the reason in a short synthesis note alongside the base artifact, including the cross-judge's verdict.\\n\\n## Phase E: Graft\\n\\nWalk each losing candidate once more and identify what is worth porting into the base. The signal is usually one or two things per candidate, not most of it.\\n\\nFold each graft in by hand, per [redesign-from-first-principles](../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md). Don't paste mechanically. The result has to remain coherent under one mental model.\\n\\nRecord what was grafted, from which candidate, and what was rejected and why.\\n\\nWhen N candidates converge on the same shape, that is a strong agreement signal. Note the convergence in the record and deliver the consensus shape within authorized scope. No graft is needed. When N candidates wildly diverge, Phase A was under-specified. Reframe and re-run rather than averaging the divergence.\\n\\n## Phase F: Verify\\n\\nThe synthesized artifact has to hold up under the same scrutiny as any other output, per [prove-it-works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\\n\\nIf verification surfaces a problem the arena did not catch, either Phase A was wrong (re-frame and re-run) or one candidate caught it and you missed the graft (go back to Phase E). Don't paper over.\\n\\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to the synthesis prose. External publication, commits, messages and setup require their own authorization.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nOne synthesized artifact. One short synthesis note alongside, naming the base, the grafts and their source candidates, the rejections, the dropouts if any, and the verification result.\\n---\\nname: interrogate\\ndescription: \\\"Use for \\\\\\\"interrogate\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"adversarial review\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"multi-model review\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"challenge this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"stress test this code\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"find blind spots\\\\\\\", or \\\\\\\"tear this apart\\\\\\\". Multiple LLM reviewers challenge changes from independent angles.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Interrogate\\n\\nSpawn one reviewer per configured model to adversarially review code changes. Each model gets the same prompt and rubric. The adversarial signal comes from model diversity, not assigned personas.\\n\\nThe deliverable is a synthesized verdict. Do NOT auto-apply changes.\\n\\n## Step 1, Determine Scope\\n\\nIdentify what to review from context:\\n\\n- If the user points at specific files or a diff, use that\\n- If on a feature branch, run `git diff main...HEAD` (or the appropriate base branch) for the full changeset\\n- If the user's message references recent work, gather the relevant files\\n\\nPackage the diff (or file contents) plus any surrounding context files the reviewers need to understand the code.\\n\\n## Step 2, State the Intent\\n\\nBefore spawning reviewers, state the intent explicitly. Derive this from:\\n\\n- The user's message\\n- Commit messages\\n- PR description if one exists\\n- The code itself\\n\\nWrite one clear paragraph. If you're unsure about the intent, ask the user before proceeding.\\n\\n## Step 3, Spawn Reviewers\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model selection](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Launch authorized independent read-only reviewers together through the exposed worker operation, one per explicitly configured reviewer selection. Extend or shrink Reviewer A/B labels to the selection count. Give each an immutable assignment, the same input revision and separate result ownership. Inspect actual results before synthesis.\\n\\nThe canonical source used a local model rule and default requests from the Claude and Grok families. The exact historical defaults are preserved as provenance in the [adaptation record](../../docs/parity/adaptations/interrogate.json). These historical requests do not establish available models. Preserve explicit session selections. With no configured selections, request two inherited reviewers and disclose that inherited or identical models do not establish model diversity. For auto or inherit-parent, omit explicit model selection. Never treat aliases as rejected model names.\\n\\nSelect explicit models and effort only from current exposed tool support. If a configured request is rejected, disclose it and use an authorized supported alternative, preferring the same family and reasoning tier where available. If the exact selection is required, report blocked. Record requested and observed models separately; missing observations remain unknown. Do not open a PR or change settings to repair upstream defaults without explicit authorization.\\n\\nWhen independent workers are unavailable, apply the same prompt and rubric sequentially with authorization and label reduced capability. Do not call repeated passes independent reviewers or consensus. Missing observed model diversity limits that claim even when worker independence succeeds. Denied review or missing source blocks grounded findings. Do not install tools or configure agents as an incidental step.\\n\\nRead `references/reviewer-prompt.md` and fill in the template with:\\n1. The stated intent\\n2. The diff or file contents\\n3. The review rubric from `references/rubric.md`\\n4. The code-quality lens from `references/code-quality-review.md`\\n\\nThe same filled template goes to all reviewers, so every model applies the code-quality lens.\\n\\n## Step 4, Synthesize\\n\\nAs results come back, build a unified picture:\\n\\n1. **Parse all findings** from the reviewers\\n2. **Identify consensus**. Findings raised by 2+ models independently are highest signal.\\n3. **Identify lone-model findings**. Still worth reading, but weight accordingly.\\n4. **Deduplicate**. Different models may describe the same issue differently. Merge these and note which models raised it.\\n5. **Note disagreements**. If one model flags something and another explicitly says the opposite, that's useful context for the verdict.\\n\\n## Step 5, Lead Judgment\\n\\nYou are the lead reviewer, a pragmatic senior engineer, not a neutral aggregator.\\n\\nRead `references/lead-judgment.md` for the full framework.\\n\\nCategorize every finding using these buckets:\\n\\n- **Act on**. Real issues affecting correctness, security, or maintainability given the actual goals. These would block a real PR.\\n- **Consider**. Legitimate points, but you're not sure they outweigh the cost of addressing them right now. Worth the user's attention.\\n- **Noted**. Technically valid but not actionable. Context-dependent, premature optimization, or low-impact given the current stage.\\n- **Dismissed**. Wrong, nitpicky, or missing context. Brief explanation why.\\n\\nFor each finding, include:\\n- Which model(s) raised it\\n- The category (act on / consider / noted / dismissed)\\n- A one-line rationale for the categorization\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nPresent the verdict in this structure:\\n\\n### Intent\\n> [The stated intent paragraph from Step 2]\\n\\n### Reviewers\\nReport requested and observed models, worker independence, model diversity and capability limits. Missing observations remain unknown.\\n\\n- Reviewer [label]: [model name], [N findings] (one bullet per reviewer)\\n\\n### Act On\\n[Findings that should be addressed. For each: description, which models raised it, why it matters.]\\n\\n### Consider\\n[Findings worth thinking about. For each: description, which models raised it, tradeoff involved.]\\n\\n### Noted\\n[Valid but low-priority. Brief list.]\\n\\n### Dismissed\\n[Rejected findings with brief rationale.]\\n\\n### Agreement Map\\n[Where did models agree, where did they diverge, and what does the pattern of agreement/disagreement tell us?]\\n# Reviewer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each reviewer subagent's prompt from this template, filling in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are an adversarial code reviewer. Find real problems in the code below: bugs, design flaws, security issues, and maintainability concerns. You are not here to be helpful or encouraging. You are here to stress-test.\\n\\n## Intent\\n\\nThe author's stated intent for this change:\\n\\n> {INTENT}\\n\\nYou are reviewing whether the code achieves this intent well. Do NOT question the intent itself. Assume the goal is correct and challenge the execution.\\n\\n## Code Under Review\\n\\n{DIFF_OR_FILES}\\n\\n## Review Rubric\\n\\n{RUBRIC_CONTENTS}\\n\\n## Code Quality Lens\\n\\n{CODE_QUALITY_CONTENTS}\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nReview the code through every lens in the rubric and the code-quality lens above that you find relevant. Do not force lenses that don't apply. A simple bug fix does not need paragraphs about architectural integrity.\\n\\nFor each finding, provide:\\n\\n1. **Severity**: `critical` | `warning` | `nit`\\n   - `critical`: Would cause bugs, data loss, security issues, or fundamentally broken behavior\\n   - `warning`: Design concern, maintainability risk, or correctness issue that isn't immediately broken but will cause pain\\n   - `nit`: Style, naming, minor improvement.\\n2. **Finding**: What the problem is, in concrete terms. Reference specific lines/functions.\\n3. **Evidence**: Why you believe this is a problem. Show your reasoning. Don't just assert.\\n4. **Suggestion** (optional): What you'd do instead, if you have a concrete alternative. Skip this if you don't have a clear fix.\\n\\n## What Makes a Good Finding\\n\\n- It references specific code, not vague concerns (\\\"this could be better\\\")\\n- It explains WHY something is a problem, not just THAT it is\\n- It distinguishes between \\\"this is broken\\\" and \\\"I would have done this differently\\\"\\n- It considers the stated intent. A finding that ignores the context of what's being built is a bad finding\\n\\n## What to Avoid\\n\\n- Restating what the code does without identifying a problem\\n- Praising the code. You're an adversary, not a cheerleader. If you find nothing wrong, say \\\"no findings\\\" and stop.\\n\\n## Output\\n\\nReturn your findings as a structured list. If you have zero findings, say so. An empty review is a valid outcome.\\n\\n```\\n## Findings\\n\\n### 1. [Severity] Short title\\n**Location**: file:line or function name\\n**Finding**: What's wrong\\n**Evidence**: Why this matters\\n**Suggestion**: (optional) What to do instead\\n\\n### 2. [Severity] Short title\\n...\\n```\\n# Review Rubric\\n\\nReview through whichever lenses are relevant. Not every lens applies to every change. Use judgment.\\n\\n## Correctness\\n\\nDoes the code actually do what the intent says it should?\\n\\n- Edge cases: empty inputs, nil/undefined, boundary values, concurrent access\\n- Error handling: are errors caught, propagated, or silently swallowed?\\n- Off-by-one, type coercion, integer overflow, string encoding\\n- State management: race conditions, stale closures, dangling references\\n- Does the happy path work? Does the sad path work?\\n- Idempotency: what happens if this operation runs twice, or if a previous run crashed halfway? If the answer is \\\"it depends on what state was left behind,\\\" there's a missing reconciliation step.\\n- Concurrency: if multiple actors can touch the same mutable state (files, branches, shared data), is access serialized structurally (locks, sequential phases, exclusive ownership), or by conventions that won't hold?\\n\\nWhen you find a potential bug, trace the execution path. Don't just flag \\\"this could be nil\\\". Show the call chain that makes it nil.\\n\\n## Root Causes vs. Symptoms\\n\\nIs the code fixing the actual problem or papering over a symptom?\\n\\nAnswering this often requires looking beyond the changed files. Read the surrounding code (callers, callees, type definitions, sibling modules) and understand the architecture the change lives in. Use the tools available to you (Read, Grep, Glob) to explore. Follow the call chain. Read the types. Understand why the code exists before judging whether the change addresses the right layer.\\n\\n- Guard clauses that mask a deeper invariant violation\\n- Retry logic that hides a broken contract\\n- Type casts that silence a modeling error\\n- If you see a workaround, ask: why is the workaround needed? What would a proper fix look like?\\n- A fix in module A that should really be a fix in module B's contract\\n- Instructions where structure would be better: if the fix is a comment saying \\\"don't do X\\\" or a convention someone has to remember, ask whether it could instead be a type constraint, a lint rule, or a runtime check that makes the wrong thing impossible\\n\\n## Structural Integrity\\n\\nDoes the code fit well into the system it's part of?\\n\\n- Boundary discipline: is validation at system boundaries, or scattered through business logic? Validate data once where it enters the system, then trust it internally.\\n- Abstraction level: is the code mixing high-level orchestration with low-level detail?\\n- Coupling: does this change introduce dependencies that will make future changes harder?\\n- Data model fit: do the data structures match the actual access patterns? The right structure makes downstream code obvious. The wrong one fights you at every turn.\\n- Bolted-on vs. integrated: was the change patched onto the existing design, or does it read as if the design always accounted for it? If the new requirement had been known from the start, would the code look like this?\\n- Legacy dual-paths: does the change introduce a new API while keeping the old one alive? If there are no external consumers, migrate callers and delete the old path in the same wave. Don't leave compatibility layers that will become permanent.\\n\\nDon't penalize simple code for lacking abstraction. Premature abstraction is worse than duplication.\\n\\n## Verification\\n\\nCan you tell that this code works from reading it?\\n\\n- Are there tests? Do they test behavior or implementation details?\\n- Are there assertions/invariants that would catch regressions?\\n- If this is a bug fix: is there a test for the bug?\\n- If this touches an integration boundary: is the full path tested?\\n- Check the real thing, not a proxy. If the code checks liveness via file mtime or cached state instead of reading the actual value, that's a verification gap.\\n- For delegated or async work: does the code verify actual output artifacts, or does it trust self-reports and summaries?\\n\\n## Complexity Budget\\n\\nIs the complexity justified by what the code accomplishes?\\n\\n- Code that could be simpler without losing correctness or clarity\\n- Abstractions that serve only one call site\\n- Configuration or parameterization for cases that don't exist yet\\n- Dead code, unused imports, vestigial parameters\\n- Over-engineering: \\\"just in case\\\" code paths with no current callers\\n- Obsolete compatibility paths kept alive for transitional stability that's no longer needed. If the migration is done, delete the scaffolding\\n- Does the user experience justify the complexity? Every feature, control, and option should earn its place. Half-finished features are worse than missing ones.\\n\\nSimpler is better unless simpler is wrong. Three lines of duplication beat a premature abstraction.\\n\\n## Security\\n\\nFor each security finding, trace the input path through the code and show it.\\n\\n- User input flowing to dangerous sinks (SQL, shell, eval, innerHTML) without sanitization\\n- Authentication/authorization gaps in new endpoints\\n- Secrets in code, logs, or error messages\\n- TOCTOU (time-of-check-time-of-use) in security-critical paths\\n---\\nname: principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Separate Before Serializing Shared State\\n\\nWhen concurrent actors might share mutable state, first ask whether they need the same mutable object. If not, eliminate the sharing. When sharing is real, enforce serialization structurally: lockfiles, sequential phases, exclusive ownership. Instructions and conventions are not concurrency control.\\n\\n**Why:** Concurrent writes to shared state create race conditions that are intermittent, hard to reproduce, and expensive to debug.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n1. **Identify shared mutable state** (files both read and write, branches both push to, APIs both define and consume).\\n2. **Default: eliminate the shared write target.** Ask: do these actors need one canonical object, or are they publishing independent facts? Give each actor its own owned file, key, branch, or state directory, and merge only at the read/reporting boundary. Two workers writing their own `lastX` field into one `state.json` is still shared mutation. `indexer-state.json` + `metrics-state.json` is not.\\n3. **Only when one shared write target is a real invariant, serialize access structurally** (lockfiles, sequential phases, single-writer actor, or atomic compare-and-swap). Treat \\\"we need a lock\\\" as a design smell to check, not as the default answer.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nBefore dispatching writers or changing shared state, check [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) and follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Assign distinct owned paths before writes start. Isolation does not grant permission to spawn workers, write files or perform external actions.\\n\\nIf execution or writes are unavailable or denied, state the missing requirement and provide the isolation design without claiming implementation. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. A design or sequential run does not prove independent workers or prevention of concurrent races.\\nexport function visible(orders,status){return orders.filter(o=>o.status===status)}\\nexport function exportCsv(orders){return \\\"id,customer,total\\\\n\\\"+orders.map(o=>`${o.id},${o.customer},${o.total}`).join(\\\"\\\\n\\\")}\\n[\\n  {\\n    \\\"id\\\": 1,\\n    \\\"status\\\": \\\"open\\\",\\n    \\\"customer\\\": \\\"Ada, Inc.\\\",\\n    \\\"total\\\": 12.5\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"id\\\": 2,\\n    \\\"status\\\": \\\"closed\\\",\\n    \\\"customer\\\": \\\"Hidden\\\",\\n    \\\"total\\\": 4\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"id\\\": 3,\\n    \\\"status\\\": \\\"open\\\",\\n    \\\"customer\\\": \\\"Say \\\\\\\"Hi\\\\\\\"\\\",\\n    \\\"total\\\": 0\\n  }\\n]\"}}\n"

CSV runtime tests observe hidden/closed exclusion, displayed column order, comma/quote/newline escaping and useful empty return feedback. I inspected complete actual diff/source and corrected one real compatibility issue: exportCsv must preserve the already-filtered supplied rows rather than silently defaulting them to open status. Final lead Bun replay observed7 tests and8 assertions green. Added local static dashboard with filter, table, Export button and empty message. Browser rendering, actual download and interactions remain unverified. Alternative A snapshot was selected over B stateful controller because it removes lifecycle coordination; comparison was sequential, not full arena. The next maintainer inherits one shared snapshot and column list.\",\"limitations\":[\"Throughput checkpoint: blocking source/data-shape/baseline gates before implementation; independent design artifacts disjoint while CSV and UI stay one code-coupled owner; browser filter and snapshot have one writer with no shared cache; smallest safe sequence is model/CSV check, fixture check, UI wiring then lead review/replay. Ordered implementation units executed accordingly. Variants: normal independent code-owner task completed and lead inspected. Workers-unavailable repeat remains UNSTARTED under root scheduling correction and takeover, rather than being silently treated as satisfied by lead edits. Matching-browser-denied branch is BLOCKED at absent matching control-ui companion; no denied browser request, screenshots or interactions fabricated. Mandatory independent architecture candidates, fresh cross-judge, full arena, interrogate, deslop and no-comments reviews remain unsatisfied. Requested selections inherited; actual models unknown. Opening-a-pr.md is present/read, but commit/rebase/push/PR/external actions unauthorized and unperformed. Page requires HTTP origin; no server started. Worker first2tool reads and logging self-calls have disclosed capture gaps. Worker report contains its own reduced-pass label; that label is not adopted as a grade or host result. This report assigns no pass grade. Leaf sequence-verifiable-units was read by lead before implementation; worker independently disclosed its later read as process-order gap.\",\"TypeScript compiler proof unverified; Bun transpilation is not compiler proof. No running application/browser proof.\",\"Raw tool request/response files cover substantive operations. Logging calls do not recursively log themselves. Initial assignment discovery and rejected patch exact request remain native conversation only; rejected patch error summary is retained. This is not a complete native transcript.\"]}\n"

    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/checklist.md",
      "bytes": 5890,
      "sha256": "5ab43bd1145f1a4b059c0faa1ff6c97adb80c0f8b14f2d499a7fe5fbbea0170c",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "### Feature\n\nYou own the design. Plan, review, verify. Delegate implementation. Stay in the lead.\n\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md) in full before work. Follow its Comments rules and required companion gates. Discover authorized operations through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md). Use [assignment and integration](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md). Keep the playbook steps verbatim in an exposed todo list, or an authorized local checklist with the reduced binding disclosed. Invocation aliases name workflows, not registered host commands.\n\n1. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem. Ground the intended user outcome in traced source and caller behavior.\n2. Run [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) for parallel design exploration. Follow its complete design and arena contracts. Preserve sequential alternatives only as reduced capability when independent workers are unavailable. Required independence remains blocked.\n3. Write the throughput checkpoint as four todo items. Keep any dimension that does not apply, such as a single file or no fan-out, with `n/a: <reason>` rather than dropping it.\n   - Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\n   - Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services, or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\n   - Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Serialize only for real invariants.\n   - Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\n4. Delegate code-writing to a fresh independent worker with the entire [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and exact file pointers. Use the configured feature role when supported and observed. Otherwise request inherited model and effort. Record requested and observed selections separately, with unknown for unobserved values. Assign exact file paths, a named data shape and its organizing structure, and success criteria tied to the user outcome. Choose the structure before the delegate writes logic under [model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Prefer a state machine over scattered booleans, a table or registry over branching, or a typed model over repeated shape assumptions. When implementation admits multiple valid shapes, such as error handling, abstraction layer or test structure, delegate through [arena](../../arena/SKILL.md). Its runners surface alternatives and its fresh cross-judge guards the pick. This gate is mandatory. There is no skip-with-reason escape, and Laziness Protocol does not override it. The gain is review separation, not lines saved. A worker forbidden to spawn owns the diff directly and returns it to the lead for the same review separation. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \"standing by\". Without authorized independent workers, direct implementation can be an explicit reduced result, but it does not satisfy independent review separation. The lead reviews the actual diff and owns the summary. Apply the mode's Comments rules. Make surgical edits and re-ground upstream-derived files against their source. Port shared-primitive improvements to all consumers and verify each. Commit each verified unit when authorized and after the mode's commit gates.\n5. Verify on the matching surface using available authorized control tools and the mode's matching companion requirements. Tie observed evidence to the intended user outcome. \"Inconclusive\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it and retain the unverified gate. Compilation or structural checks do not prove the feature works.\n6. Rebase into small, ordered commits and stack follow-ups when authorized and safe for concurrent work. Use [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md). Build, verify and commit each small unit before the next. An unrun required check blocks advancement. If commit or rebase is unavailable or unauthorized, return the checked diff and ordered delivery plan with those operations unperformed.\n7. If the design is contested, run [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) before shipping. Preserve its review rubric, independence and observed model-diversity limits. Sequential review does not become independent or multi-model review.\n8. Run the Opening a PR playbook at `opening-a-pr.md` in this directory. This caller requires that complete playbook, its review gates and actual PR result inspection. If the file is absent, report the exact deferred dependency and stop PR-dependent work. Do not reconstruct it from this paragraph. External PR creation and push require actual authorization. Return a local reviewable result when those actions are unavailable or unauthorized. Never claim a local draft is a created PR. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\n\nCode-coupled work, such as one feature or one migration, goes to a single owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out internally after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for slices that produce independent artifacts, such as audits, cross-subsystem investigations or competing experiments. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries. Spawn a fresh owner rather than chaining interrupts. Distinct owned paths and inspected results remain prerequisites for integration.\n\nReply with what you built, what you chose and why, the throughput checkpoint, and open decisions. Use tables for design alternatives. Name the end user's changed behavior and what the next maintainer inherits. Link observed verification artifacts and actual PRs. Disclose missing companions, unavailable workers, unobserved models, failed checks and deferred dependencies beside the affected claim. Keep incomplete gates visible.\n"
    },

      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/work/index.html",
      "bytes": 1334,
      "sha256": "188da5c9ed7d6f55b30ffc93563ff268e146404cccb96c4641e5e49ab8a993fc",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width,initial-scale=1\">\n<title>Orders dashboard</title>\n<style>\nbody{font-family:system-ui,sans-serif;max-width:880px;margin:48px auto;padding:0 24px;color:#142537;background:#f5f7fa}main{background:white;padding:28px;border-radius:12px}header{display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:space-between;gap:20px}h1{font-size:28px}button,select{font:inherit;padding:10px;border:1px solid #bcc8d4;border-radius:6px}button{background:#1455aa;color:white;cursor:pointer}table{width:100%;border-collapse:collapse;margin-top:24px}th,td{text-align:left;padding:12px;border-bottom:1px solid #dae1e8}th{text-transform:capitalize}#message{min-height:24px;margin-top:20px}\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<main>\n<header><h1>Orders</h1><button id=\"export\" type=\"button\" disabled>Export CSV</button></header>\n<label for=\"status\">Order filter</label>\n<select id=\"status\"><option value=\"open\">Open</option><option value=\"closed\">Closed</option><option value=\"no-results\">No results</option></select>\n<table aria-label=\"Filtered orders\"><thead id=\"head\"></thead><tbody id=\"rows\"></tbody></table>\n<p id=\"message\" role=\"status\" aria-live=\"polite\">Loading orders...</p>\n</main>\n<script type=\"module\" src=\"./dashboard-ui.mjs\"></script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/work/orders.json",
      "bytes": 269,
      "sha256": "561bca1cb0c759f9785ae826d8285fab84f0d419760917709a7f44968d10ee93",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "[\n  {\n    \"id\": 1,\n    \"status\": \"open\",\n    \"customer\": \"Ada, Inc.\",\n    \"total\": 12.5\n  },\n  {\n    \"id\": 2,\n    \"status\": \"closed\",\n    \"customer\": \"Hidden\",\n    \"total\": 4\n  },\n  {\n    \"id\": 3,\n    \"status\": \"open\",\n    \"customer\": \"Say \\\"Hi\\\"\",\n    \"total\": 0\n  }\n]"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/work/fixture.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 857,
      "sha256": "e64d4493681b354c3c0efbbf6d448807966f7dfb51d7a29b3c41278c2bab95e7",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { orderView, exportView } from \"./dashboard.mjs\";\nconst orders = await Bun.file(new URL(\"./orders.json\", import.meta.url)).json();\n\ntest(\"actual fixture exports only displayed open rows with escaped customer values\", () => {\n  expect(exportView(orderView(orders, \"open\"))).toEqual({\n    kind:\"ready\",csv:'id,customer,total\\n1,\"Ada, Inc.\",12.5\\n3,\"Say \"\"Hi\"\"\",0'\n  });\n});\ntest(\"actual fixture closed selection exports just the selected row\", () => {\n  expect(exportView(orderView(orders, \"closed\"))).toEqual({\n    kind:\"ready\",csv:\"id,customer,total\\n2,Hidden,4\"\n  });\n});\ntest(\"actual fixture no-results filter returns feedback\", () => {\n  expect(exportView(orderView(orders, \"no-results\"))).toEqual({\n    kind:\"empty\",message:\"No orders match this filter. Choose another filter to export orders.\"\n  });\n});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.mjs",
      "bytes": 1020,
      "sha256": "8ad3f5ef27c9cff32e260db8901b8191680fe8149bfa6e88c4dab26effddb192",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export const columns = Object.freeze([\"id\", \"customer\", \"total\"]);\n\nexport function visible(orders, status) {\n  return orders.filter(order => order.status === status && order.hidden !== true);\n}\n\nexport function orderView(orders, status = \"open\") {\n  return { columns, rows: visible(orders, status) };\n}\n\nfunction cell(value) {\n  const text = String(value ?? \"\");\n  return /[\",\\r\\n]/.test(text) ? '\"' + text.replaceAll('\"', '\"\"') + '\"' : text;\n}\n\nexport function exportView(view) {\n  if (view.rows.length === 0) {\n    return { kind: \"empty\", message: \"No orders match this filter. Choose another filter to export orders.\" };\n  }\n  return {\n    kind: \"ready\",\n    csv: [view.columns.map(cell).join(\",\"), ...view.rows.map(order =>\n      view.columns.map(key => cell(order[key])).join(\",\")\n    )].join(\"\\n\")\n  };\n}\n\nexport function exportCsv(orders) {\n  const result = exportView({ columns, rows: orders.filter(order => order.hidden !== true) });\n  return result.kind === \"ready\" ? result.csv : columns.join(\",\") + \"\\n\";\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 1439,
      "sha256": "e770d9bc2e6d246ca30aba1ff92a47c885e22cde24d9691af7083489da18e733",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { visible, orderView, exportView, exportCsv } from \"./dashboard.mjs\";\n\ntest(\"open view excludes closed and explicitly hidden orders\", () => {\n  const rows = [\n    {id: 1, customer: \"A\", total: 0, status: \"open\"},\n    {id: 2, customer: \"Closed\", total: 4, status: \"closed\"},\n    {id: 3, customer: \"Hidden\", total: 5, status: \"open\", hidden: true}\n  ];\n  expect(visible(rows, \"open\").map(row => row.id)).toEqual([1]);\n  expect(exportView(orderView(rows, \"open\"))).toEqual({kind:\"ready\",csv:\"id,customer,total\\n1,A,0\"});\n});\n\ntest(\"quotes commas, quotes, CR and newlines while preserving displayed column order\", () => {\n  const row = {id: 7, customer: 'A, \"B\"\\r\\nC', total: 12.5};\n  expect(exportView({columns:[\"customer\",\"total\",\"id\"],rows:[row]})).toEqual({\n    kind:\"ready\", csv:'customer,total,id\\n\"A, \"\"B\"\"\\r\\nC\",12.5,7'\n  });\n});\n\ntest(\"empty view supplies useful export feedback\", () => {\n  expect(exportView(orderView([], \"open\"))).toEqual({\n    kind:\"empty\",message:\"No orders match this filter. Choose another filter to export orders.\"\n  });\n});\n\ntest(\"legacy CSV caller preserves its supplied filtered rows and excludes hidden rows\", () => {\n  expect(exportCsv(visible([\n    {id:1,customer:\"A\",total:2,status:\"open\"},\n    {id:2,customer:\"B\",total:3,status:\"closed\"},\n    {id:3,customer:\"C\",total:4,status:\"closed\",hidden:true}\n  ], \"closed\"))).toBe(\"id,customer,total\\n2,B,3\");\n});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/work/dashboard-ui.mjs",
      "bytes": 2064,
      "sha256": "89b1d13982e558bd1b3777ee8cf04af0f69960143d11f51f98b5e7023fc5f070",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { orderView, exportView } from \"./dashboard.mjs\";\n\nconst filter = document.querySelector(\"#status\");\nconst head = document.querySelector(\"#head\");\nconst rows = document.querySelector(\"#rows\");\nconst message = document.querySelector(\"#message\");\nconst button = document.querySelector(\"#export\");\n\ntry {\n  const response = await fetch(\"./orders.json\");\n  if (!response.ok) throw new Error(\"Orders could not be loaded.\");\n  const orders = await response.json();\n  let view;\n\n  function render() {\n    view = orderView(orders, filter.value);\n    const heading = document.createElement(\"tr\");\n    heading.replaceChildren(...view.columns.map(key => {\n      const cell = document.createElement(\"th\");\n      cell.scope = \"col\";\n      cell.textContent = key;\n      return cell;\n    }));\n    head.replaceChildren(heading);\n    rows.replaceChildren(...view.rows.map(order => {\n      const row = document.createElement(\"tr\");\n      row.replaceChildren(...view.columns.map(key => {\n        const cell = document.createElement(\"td\");\n        cell.textContent = String(order[key] ?? \"\");\n        return cell;\n      }));\n      return row;\n    }));\n    const result = exportView(view);\n    message.textContent = result.kind === \"empty\" ? result.message : view.rows.length + \" orders shown.\";\n  }\n\n  filter.addEventListener(\"change\", render);\n  button.addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n    const result = exportView(view);\n    if (result.kind === \"empty\") {\n      message.textContent = result.message;\n      return;\n    }\n    const url = URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([result.csv], {type: \"text/csv;charset=utf-8\"}));\n    const link = document.createElement(\"a\");\n    link.href = url;\n    link.download = \"orders-\" + filter.value + \".csv\";\n    document.body.append(link);\n    link.click();\n    link.remove();\n    setTimeout(() => URL.revokeObjectURL(url), 0);\n    message.textContent = \"Exported \" + view.rows.length + \" orders.\";\n  });\n  render();\n  button.disabled = false;\n} catch (error) {\n  message.textContent = \"Unable to load orders. Reload the page to try again.\";\n}\n"
    },

      "text": "export function calculate(rows){return rows.reduce((n,r)=>n+r.qty*r.unitPrice,0)}\nexport function adapter(invoice){return calculate(invoice.rows)}\nexport function facade(invoice){return adapter(invoice)}\nexport function controller(invoice){return {total:facade(invoice)}}\nexport function format(n){if(!Number.isFinite(n))throw new Error(\"nonfinite\");return n.toFixed(2)}\n"

      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/no-comments/SKILL.md; cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md\",\"max_output_tokens\":15000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"597ed1\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000034124,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":3823,\"output\":\"---\\nname: no-comments\\ndescription: \\\"Spawn Comment Sicko, fix accepted findings, and offer encodings for claimed constraints.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# No comments\\n\\nSpawn Comment Sicko. Act on accepted findings.\\n\\nDefer to Comment Sicko's fresh perspective.\\n\\n## Scope\\n\\nUse the caller's files or diff. Otherwise use the current diff against the base branch, default `main`, including the working tree.\\n\\n## Steps\\n\\n1. Discover the independent-worker tools exposed in this session through [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Spawn an independent worker with the [Comment Sicko role](../../references/agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) under the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), following [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Pass the scope. Do not restate its rules.\\n2. Inspect its report and diff. Reject application-code edits, scope escapes, exception-protected deletions, misstated `MUST KILL` reasons, and flags that treat kept intentional code as guilty. Reshape flags on our-code surprises stay actionable. Do not restore those comments. A keep survives only with proof it is about something we cannot change. Audit missed scoped lint and TypeScript suppressions. Correctness or safety suppressions stay actionable `MUST KILL`s. Restore deletions only with exact exceptions and scoped proof. Before accepting thin `IMPORTANT` or `do not remove` kills or keeps, run `/how` or `/why` using [how](../how/SKILL.md) or [why](../why/SKILL.md) on their symbol. If a kill is ambiguous, do not restore. If a keep is refuted or still ambiguous, delete it. Revert and rerun one rejected report with the failure named. Reject a second, report it open, and fail `/no-comments`.\\n3. Fix trivial accepted flags directly by deleting a dead path, dropping a parameter, or using the real API. If any fix needs a shape, run `/architect` using [architect](../architect/SKILL.md) once for the accepted set and surrounding code. Stop at the sketch. Architect shapes. Step 4 implements.\\n4. Implement the smallest root-cause fix in scope. Remove every named workaround. If the root cause is out of scope, land the smallest in-scope fix and report the rest open. The [principle-fix-root-causes](../principle-fix-root-causes/SKILL.md) and [principle-redesign-from-first-principles](../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md) skills guide intent only. Neither authorizes widening the fence nor fixing instances outside it. Never bolt on symptom guards.\\n5. Constraint comments say `do not remove`, `do not change wording`, or `talk to X before changing`. Leave keeps about things we cannot change. Offer the cheapest in-scope type, runtime, test, or CI lint. Wait for interactive approval. Unattended and eval require caller pre-approval. If approved, encode then delete. Otherwise delete, report the constraint open, and sketch out-of-scope work.\\n6. Report the deletion count, restored comments, reruns, architect sketch, fixes, encoding offers, encodings, unenforced constraints, and other open work.\\n\\n## Capability and permission limits\\n\\nThe Comment Sicko role edits only scoped comments when writes are authorized and available. Its final response is a report; that format does not prohibit its comment edits. This coordinator reviews those edits and implements accepted application-code fixes in steps 3 and 4, within caller authorization and scope. Verify edits before reporting deletion counts. Legal and license headers, justified public API contracts, and the role's other proven exceptions survive.\\n\\nIf independent workers are unavailable, use an authorized sequential review as reduced capability, retaining the same scope, investigations and review rules. Authorized scoped comment deletion and accepted fixes can still proceed. Do not claim a fresh independent perspective or a worker result.\\n\\nIf writes are denied, unavailable or unauthorized, use an authorized reduced read-only review. Report proposed deletions and fixes, actual deletion count zero, and unimplemented encodings and constraints. If actual edits are required and no reduction is authorized, report blocked. Missing scope, base branch, role or required investigative skill is a stated limit. Do not invent inspection or verdicts; report affected findings as skips and leave unresolved claims open.\\n\\nStep 5 does not grant write permission. Without encoding approval, delete the unprotected comment only when scoped comment writes are authorized and available; otherwise report its proposed deletion and the constraint open. Unattended or eval execution never supplies approval by itself.\\n---\\nname: technical-writing\\ndescription: \\\"Layered technical-writing standard: Diátaxis structure, Google developer style sentences, STE instruction rules, Global English syntax. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Technical writing\\n\\nThe goal is writing a tired engineer understands on the first read. Four layers get you there, one question each: what kind of document is this, how do sentences address the reader, how much does each sentence carry, and can any sentence be read two ways. Apply all four.\\n\\nThree rules sit above the layers:\\n\\n- **Cut every word that does no work.** If the sentence survives without a word, the word goes. \\\"In order to\\\" is \\\"to\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" is nothing.\\n- **Use the short, everyday word.** \\\"Use\\\", not \\\"utilize\\\". \\\"Help\\\", not \\\"facilitate\\\". \\\"Do\\\", not \\\"perform\\\". A long word has to buy its length with precision.\\n- **When a rule makes a sentence worse, fix the sentence another way or leave it alone.** The rules serve the reader. A sentence that follows every rule and sounds like a machine wrote it has failed.\\n\\nThe codebase is the word list. Write the real symbol, file, flag, or command name, not a synonym or a description of it.\\n\\nDon't invent jargon. Use the words a developer would say out loud: \\\"move\\\", \\\"delete\\\", \\\"a budget that only decreases\\\", not \\\"evacuate\\\", \\\"ratchet\\\", or \\\"endgame\\\". A named pattern is fine when the doc says what it means the first time. Propose a new offender and its replacement as an addition to `unslop`'s abstract-metaphor rule in your reply, with the diff. Don't edit that skill.\\n\\n## Vary the rhythm\\n\\nThe layers decide what a document says and how much each sentence carries. A doc can obey all of them and still read machine-written: every sentence clipped short, no view anywhere, nothing specific.\\n\\n- Mix sentence lengths on purpose. Short sentences land a point. Longer ones that take their time carry a fact with its condition or consequence.\\n- One thought per sentence does not mean one length per sentence. Split the sentence that carries two thoughts. Keep the long sentence that carries one.\\n- Have a view where the mode allows it. Explanation weighs trade-offs, so say what you make of them instead of listing pros and cons. Reference stays dry.\\n- Be specific over sterile. Not \\\"schema changes can cause issues\\\" but \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\".\\n\\n## Pick the mode first (Diátaxis)\\n\\nOne document, one mode. Two questions pick it: does the content inform action (doing) or understanding (thinking), and does it serve learning or work?\\n\\n- Action + learning: **tutorial**.\\n- Action + work: **how-to**.\\n- Understanding + work: **reference**.\\n- Understanding + learning: **explanation**.\\n\\nUse the compass on a whole document or on one sentence.\\n\\n**Tutorial: learning by doing.** You are the teacher. The learner's success is your job, not theirs. Open by saying what the learner will build, not what they will \\\"learn\\\". Every step produces a visible result, early and often. Tell them what they should see: the expected output, the prompt change, the log line. Cut explanation to one clause and a link. Teaching pauses break the lesson. Stay concrete. Write as \\\"we\\\", in commands: \\\"First, do x. Now, do y.\\\"\\n\\n**How-to: steps to a goal.** Solve a problem a person has, not an operation the machine can perform. Assume competence. Skip teaching. Action only: no digressions, no background, no completeness for its own sake. Link those instead. Allow forks and judgment: \\\"If you want x, do y.\\\" Name the guide by the task: \\\"How to calibrate the radar array\\\", not \\\"Radar array calibration\\\".\\n\\n**Reference: facts for lookup.** Describe. Only describe. No instruction, no persuasion, no opinion. Be dry, complete, and sure. State facts, options, limits, and errors with no hedging. Mirror the structure of the thing described, so code and docs can be navigated together. Put material where readers expect it. Generate from code where possible, so it stays true.\\n\\n**Explanation: understanding and why.** One bounded topic, readable away from the product. Each title should tolerate an implicit \\\"About...\\\" in front. Anchor on a real why question. Give context: design decisions, history, constraints, alternatives. Opinion is allowed here and nowhere else.\\n\\nDon't mix modes: no reference tables inside a tutorial, no tutorial hand-holding inside reference, no arguing inside a how-to. Split and link instead.\\n\\n## Write sentences to the reader (Google developer style)\\n\\n- Talk to the reader as \\\"you\\\", in the present tense. \\\"Will\\\" only for things that genuinely happen later.\\n- Say who does what: \\\"the compiler checks\\\", not \\\"is checked\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or beside the point.\\n- Write instructions as commands: \\\"Click Submit.\\\" State facts plainly. Never \\\"should be done\\\".\\n- Put the condition before the instruction: \\\"To delete the document, click Delete.\\\" The reader skips what does not apply.\\n- Put the common case first. Exceptions after.\\n- Sound like a knowledgeable friend. No buzzwords, no figurative language, no \\\"please\\\" in instructions, and never \\\"simply\\\", \\\"easy\\\", or \\\"quickly\\\" in a procedure. If it were simple, the reader would not be here.\\n- Don't pre-announce (\\\"we will soon support...\\\") and don't start consecutive sentences with the same phrase.\\n- Link with words that say where the link goes: the page title or a short description. Never \\\"click here\\\". Prefer a sentence of context on the page over a link off it.\\n- Headings carry the point, not just the topic (\\\"Pick the mode first\\\", not \\\"Modes\\\"). Sentence case. A task heading is a bare verb phrase (\\\"Create an instance\\\"). A concept heading is a noun phrase. One h1 per page, no skipped levels.\\n- Numbered lists for sequences, bullets for everything else. Introduce a list with a complete sentence. Keep items parallel.\\n- Code goes in code font. UI elements go in bold. Use serial commas. Drop \\\"etc.\\\" and say up front that a list is partial.\\n\\n## Make statements load one at a time (STE rules)\\n\\n- One instruction per sentence. One thought per sentence everywhere else.\\n- Split instructions longer than about 20 words and other sentences longer than about 25.\\n- Put the warning or condition before the step it guards: \\\"If hot oil touches your skin, injuries can occur.\\\"\\n- Keep \\\"the\\\" and \\\"a\\\": \\\"Remove backup file\\\" reads two ways. \\\"Remove the backup file\\\" reads one.\\n- Give each word one meaning and one job, then keep it. If \\\"check\\\" means inspect, don't also use it for restrain.\\n- Pick one word per action and stick to it: \\\"start\\\", not \\\"start\\\" here and \\\"initiate\\\" there.\\n- Write procedures as direct commands, never as narration and never in the passive: \\\"Install the component\\\", not \\\"the component must be installed\\\".\\n- Avoid \\\"-ing\\\" words where you can. They take too many grammatical jobs and breed misreadings.\\n\\n## Leave no sentence open to two readings (Global English)\\n\\n- Keep words like \\\"only\\\" and \\\"not\\\" next to the word they change: \\\"only fails on growth\\\" and \\\"fails only on growth\\\" say different things.\\n- Break up long noun strings: \\\"the proto import budget check script\\\" becomes \\\"the script that checks the proto-import budget\\\".\\n- Make every \\\"it\\\", \\\"they\\\", and \\\"this\\\" point at one obvious thing. Repeat the noun when in doubt. Never use \\\"this\\\" or \\\"which\\\" to point at a whole clause.\\n- Don't drop verbs: \\\"Phase 1 moves the converters and Phase 2 the runtime\\\" leaves Phase 2 without one. Give it one.\\n- Keep the small words that show structure. \\\"Ensure that the switch is off\\\" keeps \\\"that\\\" because it makes the sentence parse one way. Never trade clarity for word count.\\n- Repeat the article in a series when it prevents a misread: \\\"the client and the host\\\", not \\\"the client and host\\\", when they are two things.\\n- Say which parts \\\"and\\\" or \\\"or\\\" joins when a sentence can group two ways. \\\"Both...and\\\", \\\"either...or\\\", and \\\"if...then\\\" are free disambiguators.\\n- Use periods, not semicolons. Replace an em dash with a new sentence.\\n- Make text in parentheses a full grammatical unit or its own sentence. Never form plurals with \\\"(s)\\\".\\n- No slashes: write \\\"a, b, or both\\\" instead of \\\"a/b\\\" or \\\"and/or\\\".\\n- Call each thing by one name, everywhere. A doc that says \\\"the gate\\\", \\\"the ratchet\\\", and \\\"the budget check\\\" for one thing teaches three things. Rewording an unchanged sentence between edits costs the same way. Don't churn what didn't change.\\n- Skip idioms, colloquialisms, Latin abbreviations, and metaphors. A non-native reader, a translator, and an agent all parse plain constructions best.\\n\\n## Voice and repo specifics\\n\\n- Apply the **unslop** skill to every doc this skill touches. That skill owns the slop-pattern catalog: AI vocabulary, filler, hedging, formatting tells.\\n- PR descriptions and commit messages are writing too. Every layer except Diátaxis applies to them. A PR body is a briefing that a reviewer can read in under a minute. Do not paste swarm logs, SHA lists, or metric tables. Link them.\\n- Product UI strings are not documentation. Use your product's copy guidelines for those.\\n- Indent code snippets with tabs. Write real paths and real symbols. Make every count or tree claim true at the commit that lands it, and include the command that regenerates it.\\n\\n## Worked example\\n\\nBefore:\\n\\n> Configuration of the proto import ratchet budget script parameters is performed via budget.json. Note that it's important to remember that running with --write, which updates the committed budget to reflect the current count, should only be done when lowering it. If exceeded, CI fails.\\n\\nAfter:\\n\\n> `budget.mjs` reads the committed budget from `budget.json` and counts the files that import protos. If the count exceeds the budget, CI fails. Run `budget.mjs --write` only to lower the budget.\\n\\n## Access and permissions\\n\\nUse the current session capabilities in `../../references/host-capabilities.md` to read documents and verify authorized edits. Supplied text is enough for a rewrite in the conversation. If document writes are unavailable or denied, return the proposed text and state that it was not saved. If the document cannot be read and no text was supplied, report the missing input. Do not invent its content. Read `../unslop/SKILL.md` before applying it.     {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/verifiable-feature/design-write.raw.json",
      "bytes": 2675,
      "sha256": "0bf676cf30290e56c2d6665185bd7924bc16e8a2ea08d87bdbb65c4cf3856020",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-verifiable-feature",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"request\":\"*** Begin Patch\\n*** Add File: /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/verifiable-feature/design/alternatives.md\\n+# Export architecture alternatives\\n+\\n+These are sequential lead designs, not independent arena candidates. Required parallel candidates and fresh cross-judge remain unsatisfied.\\n+\\n+## A. Snapshot owned by the displayed view\\n+\\n+Usage: const view = orderView(orders, status); render(view); const result = exportView(view).\\n+\\n+Shape: OrderView contains rows and ordered columns. exportView(view) returns {kind:'ready',csv} or {kind:'empty',message}. One column registry contains id,customer,total in displayed order. Both renderer and exporter iterate that registry. Filtering excludes mismatched status and hidden:true before the snapshot is constructed. Pure helpers need no class lifecycle or shared global cache.\\n+\\n+This keeps export aligned to the actual visible rows and columns through one data shape. The UI owns status and refreshes its snapshot on changes. The return variant represents empty feedback directly.\\n+\\n+## B. Stateful controller owns filter and export\\n+\\n+Usage: const dashboard = new OrdersDashboard(orders); dashboard.setStatus('open'); render(dashboard.rows(),dashboard.columns()); dashboard.exportCsv().\\n+\\n+Shape: a controller class owns source rows and current status, computes filtered rows and serializes its own ordered columns. It can notify a renderer of empty results. It hides coordination but adds mutable lifecycle state and exposes several methods that callers must sequence.\\n+\\n+## Lead synthesis\\n+\\n+Choose A as the base. It has a smaller API for the current fixture and makes the displayed snapshot the export source. Graft B's single ownership of the selected filter into the browser event handler. Reject B's class lifecycle because there is one static page and no subscription requirement. No design creates a second column list or independent export filter. Candidate screening found temporal coupling in B and no meaningful domain state that requires a class. This comparison is source-grounded but not an independent arena judgment.\\n+\\n+## Ordered units\\n+\\n+1. Build pure ordered view and CSV export. Verify exact open rows and escaping, no hidden/closed rows, and empty return value through Bun.\\n+2. Add static local dashboard UI consuming the same ordered snapshot, with filter, export button and empty message. Verify source wiring; browser interactions and screenshots remain blocked on missing control-ui companion.\\n+3. Lead inspects the returned diff and reruns behavior. Keep commit/rebase/push/PR actions unperformed because scope forbids them.\\n*** End Patch\",\"response\":{}}\n"
    },

Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-fix-root-causes/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-foundational-thinking\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-foundational-thinking\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share.
